# Prestige
Prestige ranks are a character-based mechanic that grant specific characters extra skins when they complete multiple stages. These skins provide extra stats, increasing alongside the Prestige rank, with the final rank/skin allowing that character to start with a max level passive item in addition to their increased stats.
Prestige ranks are indicated by an increasing number of stars (★) appended to the character's name. The max number of Prestige ranks a character can have is 5, attained by completing 4 stages.
- Note that 4 separate stages must be completed; one cannot repeat the same stage to gain more Prestige ranks from it.
## Characters
Currently, only 2 characters utilize Prestige ranks:
-  Gav'Et-Oni ★ [DLC]
- Gav'Et-Oni ★'s Prestige ranks grant extra  Max Health,  Duration, and  Amount, with their final Prestige rank allowing them to start with a max level  Spellbinder
-  Jaman Jato ★ [DLC]
- Jaman Jato ★'s Prestige ranks grant extra  Max Health,  Duration, and  Amount, with their final Prestige rank allowing them to start with a max level  Duplicator
=====
# Random Events
After collecting the Trisection which is located in Astral Stair, the player can choose to turn on "Random Events" in the level select screen. This option can be found in a new extra window to the left of the normal stage modifiers. 
Activating this will cause a three-ring roulette of the different masks of The Directer to appear at the bottom right corner and select a random event for the player. This happens at every XX:30 mark, where the three rings will start rotating and selecting a random event, which will then trigger at the next XX:00 mark.
This effect is turned on by default on Astral Stair, until the player picks up the Trisection, after which the stage can also be played without random events.
The exact number of possible events is not yet certain, but some of these events include:
| Event | Effect
| Pet | Creates a big ring of hearts around the player, each healing them for 2 hit points.
| Candybox | Puts a Candybox on the floor near the player, enabling them to add any weapon they want to their inventory.
| Firebreathing | Gives the player the Nduja Fritta Tanto powerup.
| Gold Fever | Starts a Gold Fever.
| Pile Assault | Summons a ring of Twin Snakes around the player that shoot fireballs at them, just like in Dairy Plant. Disappears after 1 minute.
| Mino Rush | The player gets rushed by several groups of Mignotaurs, just like in Dairy Plant, for ~30 seconds.
| Scorpion Lair | Summons rows of big Twin Skulls to the left and right of the player. They disappear after 1 minute.
| Rosary | Activates the Rosary effect, killing every enemy on screen, unless they are immune to it.
| Flower Wall | Summons a circular Flower Wall around the player that slowly closes in on them. Disappears after ~50 seconds.
| Panic Time | Summons several rings of Poltergeists (both candelabra and gem-like) and Red Shades around the player that close in on them.
| Free Passive | Puts a random passive item near the player. It is currently unknown if any items are excluded.
| Summon II Molise | Replaces all enemies with random plant enemies from Il Molise. These don't move and don't give a lot of experience points. Lasts for ~2 minutes.
| The Drowner | Summons the Drowner, who fills the stage with damaging water from the bottom up, for ~1 minute.
| Light Sources | Similar to the random effect of Greatest Jubilee, creates several Light Sources around the player. They can contain random items (unless in Bone Zone) or gold.
| Roulette | Spawns a ring of poltergeists that self-destruct when the player approaches them.
| Jelly Wall | Summons a ring of Jellyfish surrounding the player that closes in and moves away from them.
| The Stalker | Summons the Stalker that slowly follows the player for ~2 minutes. Greatly speeds up at fixed intervals and deals very high damage.
| Ultra Wave | Increases the enemy spawn rate for 1 minute.
| Chicken Bonanza | Creates a ring of Floor Chickens around the player, each one healing for 30 hit points.
| Crab Fest | Summons hordes of Non-Giant Enemy Crabs that run towards the player in a fixed direction.
| Shooting Stars | Summons groups of shooting stars at the player.
| Luck Boost | Permanently increases the player's luck by 50%.
| Summon Night | Replaces all enemies with different kinds of bats for ~1 minute.
| Bat Swarm | The player gets rushed by several swarms of small bats, just like in Mad Forest, for ~30 seconds.
| Skull Swarm | Summons a swarm of Skullones that fly toward the player in a fixed direction.
| Jelly Swarm | Summons a swarm of Jellyfish that float toward the player in a fixed direction.
| Shrimp Swarm | Summons several Serpentine Dragon Shrimps that fly in wave-like patterns through the room.
| Merdusa Swarm | Summons a swarm of Sneaky Heads that fly in a fixed direction toward the player.
| Green Swarm | Summons a swarm of Musc Musc that float toward the player in a fixed direction.
| Ghost Swarm | Summons a swarm of Ghostly Apparitions that fly toward the player in a fixed direction.
| Imp Swarm | Summons a swarm of Impefingers that fly toward the player in a fixed direction.
| Skeleton Swarm | Summons a swarm of Skeletons that move toward the player in a fixed direction.
There might be more events.
=====
# Results
The Results screen appears at the end of each run and displays the statistics for that run.
In the top left, the stage name, mode, and gold bonus are displayed alongside the time survived, gold earned, level reached, and amount of enemies defeated.
In the bottom left, each weapon's stats for the run are shown, including their level, damage, amount of time used, and the damage per second (DPS). The damage and DPS columns will show one weapon each in yellow, for which weapon did the most overall damage, and the most DPS.
The damage amounts are given using SI unit prefixes. A shortened list is given below. It is noted that the last noted symbol is E for exa (meaning 1 quintillion, or 1018):
| Name | Symbol | Amount
| exa | E | 1 quintillion
| peta | P | 1 quadrillion
| tera | T | 1 trillion
| giga | G | 1 billion
| mega | M | 1 million
| kilo | k | 1 thousand
In the top right, the character name is shown and the amount of Golden Eggs used, if any.
Next a list of all the weapons and passive items picked up during the run, and their respective levels. Levels are shown in yellow if they are max, and white if they are not max.
Following the list of weapons and passives is a list of all the powerups that were active during the run, and their level.
Listed after the powerups are all the pickups and the amount of them.
Lastly is the list of arcanas and darkanas used during the run.
## Buttons
One button that always appears is the "DONE" button, which leads back to the main menu.
If the player unlocked at least one item in a run, which would be indicated at the bottom of the Results dialog, there would be a green button that allows for the player to show or hide the unlocked items/achievements. 
If the player is on mobile and has an internet connection, a green button will appear, which would allow them to watch a video advertisement to earn bonus gold.
=====
# Retaliate
Retaliate is a special attack type in Vampire Survivors. When losing health, certain weapons can retaliate. The damage of retaliation attacks is affected by  Armor.
## Effects
Retaliation attacks work differently depending on the source. Most sources of retaliatory damage require damage to be received by the player. Attacks triggered in retaliation often have a cooldown that is independent from the rest of the weapon.
 Armor will increase the damage of retaliatory damage by +10% for each point of Armor.
- Normally, this maxes out at +500% at +50 Armor. However,  Toastie can go over this limit with their Armor boost at level 200.
While the  Divine Bloodline (IX) Arcana is active, killing an enemy with retaliatory damage increases the character's  Max Health by 0.5.
The following weapons can retaliate when taking damage:
-  NO FUTURE and  Carnage Heart [DLC] (by exploding, every 0.5 seconds)
-  Victory Sword (by special triggering around the character, every 0.6 seconds)
-  Pako Battiliar and  Mazo Familiar (by extra triggering, every 1.8 seconds )
-  Crimson Shroud (by returning damage in an Area of Effect, per shield)
-  Embrace of Gaea (by extra triggering, every ? seconds)
-  Night Sword [DLC] and  Muramasa [DLC] (by special triggering around the character, every 0.6 seconds)
-  Screams from the Void [DLC] and  Forbidden Siren [DLC] (by extra triggering, every 1 second)
-  Eskizzibur [DLC] and  Legionnaire [DLC] (by extra triggering, every 1.5 seconds)
-  Iron Shield [DLC] and  Dark Iron Shield [DLC] (by firing projectiles, every 1.5 seconds)
-  Divine Bloodline (IX) (by returning damage)
-  Heart of Fire (XIX) (by exploding, every 0.5 seconds)
-  Aura Blast [DLC] as  Wind [DLC] (by extra triggering, every 1 second)
 Bracelet,  Bi-Bracelet,  Tri-Bracelet,  Kaiser Knuckle [DLC] and  Lapiste Tepisto [DLC] can retaliate when equipped by  Morris Baldwin [DLC], but this will not count as retaliatory damage.
 Stone Skull [DLC] has innate retaliation damage that is not associated with any weapon.
- Thorns has a similar effect.
 Torino starts with a hidden weapon similar to  Eskizzibur [DLC] that only does retaliatory damage and cannot evolve.
Additionally, the clones summoned by  Legionnaire [DLC], and all explosions caused by  Heart of Fire (XIX) will count as retaliatory damage, despite not always being triggered in retaliation.
=====
# Secrets
The Secrets menu in the base game, with all of the base game secrets unlocked (as of version 1.12).
The Secrets menu is unlocked after obtaining the  Forbidden Scrolls of Morbane. It shows hints to unlocking secret characters, and gives the player the ability to cast spells.
Players can temporarily unlock the Secrets menu by clicking on the ninth relic icon with the pink background (the Yellow Sign) in the collection seven times. A message saying "You are now a magician!" will appear on the tooltip, and the secret menu will now be unlocked for the current session. This allows the player to cast spells right away, instead of having to slay the  Sketamari. It must be triggered again each time the player opens the game until the relic is collected.
The following is a list of hints for finding secret characters. All secrets can be unlocked without the Forbidden Scrolls of Morbane, or using their respective spell. Once a spell for a character is used successfully in the Secrets menu, it cannot be input again, as fully typing out the spell again causes it to disappear with no effect.
## List of secrets
The code names listed here are not Spells, and will not work when typed into the Secrets menu.
Although many are similar to spells, these are the game's internal code names/IDs for the secrets.
To see the full unlock method for any listed secret, visit the page for its unlocked character.
## Base game
There are currently 23 base game secrets. The hint for Avatar Infernas only appears after defeating  The Directer for the first time.

# Showstopper
DLC Content (Emerald Diorama)
This article primarily features content only available in DLC, which may not be available in all copies of the game.
Showstopper is a mechanic unique to characters from the Emerald Diorama expansion in Vampire Survivors.
## Mechanic
Whenever a character with Showstopper reaches critical health (20% of their current Max Health), the following effect will trigger:
- Gains +100% Might, -100% Cooldown (effectively reach 10% Cooldown limit), and +100% Luck.
- All Glimmers will be triggered by every activation.
- The music will also change during Showstopper effect.
Showstopper effect lasts for 10 seconds, and can be triggered again once restored to full health.
## Tips
- Showstopper synergizes perfectly with  Crystal Cries (XII), as frozen enemies drop  Crystallized Souls that can be picked up to restore health quickly, which allows Showstopper to trigger again.
- Showstopper and the  Orologion from  Crystal Cries (XII) trigger at the same health percentage, freezing enemies and granting the player Showstopper's bonuses at the same time.
- Showstopper synergizes well with  Lifesign Scan, as it makes the cooldown effectively minimal, restoring health quickly, allowing Showstopper to trigger again. In addition, once it's evolved into  Paranormal Scan, stat boosts can be gained extremely quickly in a Showstopper state.

# Spells
This article is about the mechanic in the Secrets menu. For other uses, see Spell.
Spells are a mechanic in Vampire Survivors that allow for the usage of secret codes to unlock characters, stages, relics, and other content. Most spells must be entered in the Secrets menu, unlocked by collecting the  Forbidden Scrolls of Morbane.
Each key for every spell needs to be typed within 30 seconds of the last one for the spell to be registered.

## Trivia
- The only spells that unlock content that cannot be unlocked by some other means are "spoopyseason", "guinigi" ("guinigigi" on certain versions), "forbiddenbox", "starpupil", and "rulersword". if the game is not being played with a keyboard, "tramezzini" and "maybeimastallion" are also required.
- "x-x1viiq", "jonathancharlotte", and "ettolrahcnahtanoj" are explicitly listed or hinted at in the secrets menu, but unlock characters that can be unlocked by other methods.
- "everything" and "everywhere" can be used repeatedly, as each new update may add more content to them. Their tooltip of unlocking character is slightly different than other unlocks:
- everything: All weapons unlocked from Level Up options.
- everywhere: All main stages and hyper mode unlocked.
- These spells, along with the unlock spell for Queen Sigma ("allatonce"), are a reference to the 2022 film Everything Everywhere All at Once.
- If one enters the "spinnn" spell and closes the secrets menu while the UI is spinning, the background of the main menu will freeze and remain pivoted at an unusual angle until the spell is entered again.
- The code to unlock Exdash is a reference to the cheat code X-X!V''Q used in Castlevania: Symphony of the Night, which also gives the main hero Alucard a very high Luck stat in exchange for other stats being lower.
- The spell to unlock Il Molise is a reference to its description.
- The spell to unlock Mindbender is a reference to the quote "C'mon Teletubby, teleport us to Mars!" by Tammie Brown in RuPaul's Drag Race.
- The spell to unlock O'Sole shares its name with the credits theme of the 2006 action-adventure game Ōkami, which is called "Reset".
- The spell to unlock Gains may be a reference to the multiple awkward high fives in Final Fantasy VII Remake.
- The spell to unlock the Great Gospel is a reference to "You Can Hear the Cry of the Planet", the song which plays in the Forgotten Capital in Final Fantasy VII. The code has the "the" replaced with "captain", referencing the animated television series Captain Planet.
- The spell to unlock Gyorunton is a reference to Star Ocean: Second Evolution, a remake of the PlayStation game Star Ocean: The Second Story. One of the optional characters in the game is the main inspiration for the design and name of Gyorunton.
- The spell to unlock Leda is a reference to Sorceress Adel from Final Fantasy VIII, who, through radio interference phenomena, shows several messages at the Timber TV Station's screen, one of which is "I WILL NEVER LET YOU FORGET ABOUT ME".
- The spells “tengillesbalm”, “duraistruth”, “haeralisploy” and “lugrianstale” all reference characters from Final Fantasy Tactics: Meliadoul Tengille, Orran Durai, Delita Heiral (who receives the historical epithet ‘Haeralis the Brave’), and Ramza Beoulve (who uses the surname ‘Lugria’ to hide his identity as a mercenary).
- The spell to unlock Big Trouser is the sentence "è arrivato l'arrotino", which is a refrain spoken over loudspeakers by knife grinders in Italy as they drive around small towns to attract attention.
- The spells to unlock the Arcanas (except Sarabande of Healing (VI)) are the Italian names of their corresponding Major Arcana Cards for each of them.
- The spells to unlock the Darkanas (except Moonlight Bolero (VI)) are the spells of their corresponding Arcanas spelled backwards.
- The spells "everything", "everywhere", and "all at once" can be combined to make the name of the film Everything Everywhere All At Once.
- The spell "gottagofast" is a reference to the catchphrase of Sonic the Hedgehog.
- The spell "coldwaterhotwater" and its association with Yatta Cavallo are likely references to the manga series Ranma ½, wherein several characters are cursed to transform when doused with cold water and return to normal with hot water, with one such transformation being a panda.
- The spell "shoppingcarttheory" is a reference to an internet meme about whether someone returns the shopping cart back to the proper place or not is a judgment of their character and ethics.
- The spell "chapatribe" is a reference to the Chapa tribe, a tribe of rodent-like humanoids from Unlimited Saga.
- The spell "flymetothemoon" is a reference to the song "Fly Me to the Moon", which has a rendition in the first Bayonetta game in 2009.
- The spells "onjupiter" and "andmars" are likely also references to this song.
- The spell "ifeellovevenus" is a reference to the Blue Man Group's cover of the song "I Feel Love" by Donna Summer, featuring Venus Hum.
- The spell "yattapanda" is a reference to Yattaman, in which the naming of robots is comprised of the word "yatta" combined with a name of an animal, such as Yatta-Pesce (fish), Yatta-Can (dog), and Yatta-Panda.
- The spell "languorino" is a phrase meaning "the desire to eat something/feeling peckish" in Italian. It is tied to an Italian meme, related to a chocolate spot where a woman is a talking to her chauffer, Ambrogio.
- The spell "quandolodicelui" is a phrase meaning "when he says so" in Italian. It is a phrase related to the television personality Divino Otelma.
- The spell "igottagettotheedgeofsoul" is a lyrics in the song "The Edge of Soul", which is the theme song of the 1995 fighting game Soul Edge.
- The spell "fettinepanate" means "fried cutlets", which is an Italian meme tied to the anime Ai Shite Naito.
- The spell "kalvasflam" is a reference to the words of power spoken when casting the spell "Flamestrike" in Ultima Online.
- The spell "pinociampino" means Ciampino pine in Italian.
- The spell "earrivatolarrotino" is a reference to an Italian song "È arrivato l'arrotino" by Roberto Colombo.
- The spell "rightninetyseven" and its code name LostNumber is a reference to the original Final Fantasy VII, in which a secret code was needed to unlock a safe: "(Right 36), (Left 10), (Right 59), (Right 97)", upon inputting the last missing number into the safe (Right 97), "Lost Number" would appear.
- The spell "ionicoionico" refers to a word game where, if the word "ionico" (which means "ionic" in Italian) is continously pronounced, it can be easily confused with the word "coglioni" (which is Italian vulgar for "testicles").
- The spell "chevolano" translated from Italian into: "That fly". It can combine with Gav'Et-Oni ★ weapon name into "Mille bolle blu che volano" ("A thousand blue bubbles that fly) to complete the reference of the song "Le mille bolle blu" by Italian singer Mina.
- The spell "vogue" is a reference to American pop star Madonna's 1990 hit Vogue, itself inspired in turn by "voguing", a style of dancing in which the dancers pose themselves as if for a photograph in a fashion magazine; this reference is reinforced by Babi-Onna's walking animation cycle, which is instead just her voguing endlessly across the field.
- The spell "animeismypassion" might be a reference to the "graphic design is my passion" meme.
- The spell "rottingpizza" is a reference to how the characters of Final Fantasy VII describe the structure of Midgar.
- The spell "kamiki" and its codename FESTIVAL is a reference to the Kamiki Festival from Ōkami, where they celebrate with fireworks (hence Greatest Jubilee); Amaterasu is a goddess they worship and is the main character (O'Sole is a reference to her); she has the ability to use Celestial Brush to draw brushstrokes that affect her surrounding and when she runs she create grass and flowers (Celestial Dusting references both); Cherry Bomb is literally one of her Celestial Brush techniques; and the company producing this game is called Clover Studio, which is featured in Okami as a huge source of praise (experience) when blooming them and to reference their own studio, which is why O'Sole gets Clover and Little Clover.
- The spell "blunderingfool" is a reference to Steam user BlunderingFool, who repeatedly requested the inclusion of a mecha Gyorunton 13 times.
- The spell "whathappensifisitidleforoneday" is sent as a message in Discord by Luca pretending to be a new player, with the full message being "hi everyone I'm new to the game, does anyone know What Happens If I Sit Idle For One Day ?" where the spell part is capitalized.
- Multiple spells from the Emergency Meeting DLC reference various Among Us memes or terminology, often directed at the color of the character unlocked.
- "redissus" references the term "sus", used as an abbreviation for "suspicious". In this case, it implies somebody saw Dino, the red crewmate, acting suspiciously.
- "isaworangevent" references a common claim of seeing somebody enter a vent, an ability only an impostor has in Among Us , whether truthful or not... at least until the Engineer role was added to the game. This implies somebody saw Gino, the orange engineer, enter a vent.
- "foundyellowinmedical" and "foundblueinstorage" reference finding dead bodies in various areas on Polus. Given that Lino, a ghost, and Pina, a Guardian Angel, are dead, this implies this is where their bodies were found.
- "greenisredyouisme" is referencing the shapeshifter role, a type of impostor who can impersonate crewmates to place suspicion on them. In this case, it is implied that Nino, the green Shapeshifter, shapeshifted into Dino, the red crewmate.
- "selfreport" is referencing the act of self-reporting: an impostor reporting the body of somebody they killed, intending to seem less suspicious that way.
- Multiple spells from the Ode to Castlevania DLC reference factoids or memes from the Castlevania series, typically related to the character or item they unlock.
- The spell to unlock Christopher "firsttodoittwice" references how Cristopher was the very first Belmont to fight Dracula more than once- first in Castlevania: The Adventure and then again in Castlevania II: Belmont's Revenge.
- The spell to unlock Julius, "omniavanitas" is named for Omnia Vanitas, an evasion technique used exclusively by Julius in Castlevania: Aria of Sorrow and Castlevania: Harmony of Despair.
- The spell to unlock Soma "technicallynotavampire" references how Soma is a human, despite being the reincarnation of Dracula.
- The spell to unlock Alucard, "adrianfahrenheit" is Alucard's first and middle names. His full name is Adrian Fahrenheit Tepes.
- The spell to unlock Dracula, "notinthisgame" jokes about Dracula's status as a vampire, which Vampire Survivors previously joked about having zero of.
- The spell to unlock Megalo Dracula, "mathiascronqvist" is referring to Mathias Cronqvist, the man who would much later become Dracula.
- The spell to unlock the Pile of Secrets, "isthiswhatamanis" references the infamous Playstation version of the introduction to Castlevania: Symphony of the Night and more specifically, Dracula's line from said intro, "What is a man? A miserable little pile of secrets!".
- The spell to unlock Richter, "doesnthaveuno" references a meme made by Nova Arby (more known under Zeramations Castlevania: Rondo of Cards animation) in which Richter confronts Dracula in the same manner as the introduction to Castlevania: Symphony of the Night, but their lines are instead that of the infamous "You have UNO" meme.

# Survarot
SurvarotGrand BoosterJumbo Booster
| Survarot
| Brings up a Survarot draft.
| Pickup
| Release | 28 October 2025 (Update)
| ID | SV_DRAFT1
| Rarity | ? (edit)
| Unlock level | 0

| Effects | Brings up a Survarot draft.
| {"effects":"Brings up a Survarot draft.","luck":"","version2":"Grand Booster","version3":"Jumbo Booster","description":"Brings up a Survarot draft.","id1":"SV_DRAFT1","update":"Vampire Survivors: Ante Chamber out now, for free!","version1":"Survarot","rarity":"","image":"","name":"Survarot","id3":"SV_DRAFT3","release":"28 October 2025","level":"0","id2":"SV_DRAFT2","dlc":""}
SV_DRAFT1SV_DRAFT2SV_DRAFT3
Survarots are a mechanic in Vampire Survivors that function similarly to  Arcana, granting the player various bonuses. However, Survarots mainly affect the player's stats, along with giving simple effects. 
Survarots can be unlocked by purchasing the  Survarocchi from  Big Troubler in The Lycaeum for 120,000. After purchasing this relic, Survarots can be enabled on character select in the "Character Setup" menu under "Power Creep." Note that Survarots cannot be enabled alongside  Golden Eggs.
## Obtaining
Survarots can be obtained by:
- Breaking Light Sources as any character after enabling Survarots in the Power Creep setting before a run, unlocked with the  Survarocchi relic.
- Breaking Light Sources as  Secretino Dagsson,  Jimbo [DLC],  Chicot [DLC], and  Perkeo [DLC]
- These characters, excluding Perkeo, will also receive a Survarot draft at the start of a run.
- Using one of these characters in co-op mode or as a CPU in  Party Mode will also allow Survarots to appear.
-  Skipping a Level Up as  Canio [DLC]
- Finding them as pickups on Ante Chamber.
- Defeating a  Bat Dragon will drop one regardless of if the Power Creep setting is enabled.
When dropping from Light Sources, Survarots have a chance to replace  Gold Coins. This chance depends on how long the player has survived, and how many Survarots have dropped, including those currently on the floor. The player starts with a Survarot "limit" of 1, which increases by 1 every 5 minutes. The following chances are unaffected by  Luck:
- If the player's total Survarots are 2 or more under the limit, then the chance for a Gold Coin to be replaced is 20%.
- If the player's total Survarots are exactly 1 under the limit, then the chance for a Gold Coin to be replaced is 10%.
- If the player's total Survarots are equal to the limit, then the chance for a Gold Coin to be replaced is 2.5%.
- If the player's total Survarots are exactly 1 over the limit, then the chance for a Gold Coin to be replaced is 1%.
- If the player's total Survarots are 2, or more, over the limit, then the chance for a Gold Coin to be replaced is 0%, and a Survarot will not be able to drop for some time.
Essentially, the player is more likely to encounter Survarots as time passes, assuming the number of Survarots they possess remains unchanged.
## Drafting
Example of a Survarot Draft.
Drafting a Survarot is much like drafting an Arcana. When drafting, the player is given a choice of 4-6 Cards, with the option to skip the draft, reroll the draft, or BOOST, costing gold. Rerolling a Survarot draft will always grant random cards, unlike Arcana. The number of cards available in the first draft is determined by the type of "Booster Pack" dropped, 4 cards for normal drafts, 5 cards for Grand drafts, and 6 cards for Jumbo packs. The chances for each type of Booster Pack are 50%, 35%, and 15%, respectively, and are unaffected by  Luck. There is no indicator for the type of draft dropped before picking it up.
Two of the exact same base card will never appear in the same draft, but two of the same FOIL Skill can. 
Each card has a chance to come with 1 of 5 Editions. Cards can only have one edition.
| Image | Name | Description
| | POLYchrome | Doubles (most) starting bonuses.
| | HOLOgraphic | Survarot card gains 4 Survarot Multiplier.
| | INVErted | Inverts and halves (most) starting bonuses, but lowers Risk.
| | FOIL | Grants the card a random skill.
| | GALActic | Grants a card-specific skill.
Each slot in a draft will have a different weight for each edition. Note that these weights only apply to non-BOOSTed drafts, and slots aren't necessarily drawn from left to right (the 5th Survarot from the left will not always have an edition, for example, but one card will follow that weighting).
| Slot Number | Base | POLYchrome | HOLOgraphic | INVErted | FOIL | GALActic
| 1-4 | 75 | 7 | 7 | 3 | 4 | 4
| 5 | 0 | 7 | 7 | 0 | 4 | 4
| 6-7 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 50 | 50
## BOOSTing
Upon drafting, the player is given the option to BOOST for gold. This cost scales depending on the player's gold, but never exceeds 99,999. Whenever the player BOOSTs a draft, the cost of BOOSTing that same draft will decrease.
| Player's Coins | Initial Cost | 1 BOOST | 2 BOOSTs | 3 BOOSTs | 4 BOOSTs | 5 BOOSTs (Minimum Cost)
| 0-999,999 | 2,999 | 2,499 | 1,999 | 1,499 | 999 | 499
| 1,000,000-99,999,999 | 9,999 | 8,999 | 7,999 | 6,999 | 5,999 | 4,999
| 100,000,000 | 99,999 | 89,999 | 79,999 | 69,999 | 59,999 | 49,999
BOOSTing will always give a draft consisting of 7 cards with an Edition.
When BOOSTing, editions are weighted in the following manner:
| Base | POLYchrome | HOLOgraphic | INVErted | FOIL | GALActic
| 0 | 2 | 2 | 1 | 4 | 4
Alternatively, in percentages:
| Base | POLYchrome | HOLOgraphic | INVErted | FOIL | GALActic
| 0% | ~15.38% | ~15.38% | ~7.69% | ~30.77% | ~30.77%
## Effects
Each Survarot card has an effect unique to that card, including stat bonuses and Skills. Skills can provide a variety of effects, such as stat bonuses and granting the player weapons. Unless a Skill or Starting Bonus is specified to be lost under a certain condition, it is permanent. (Most) Skills and Starting Bonuses can stack, are not overridden upon obtaining a new card.
Skills are paired with icons that show the condition or effect:
- A stat is decreased to the player's deficit.
- Often occurs with cards and cards unlocked by  Enemahs.
- Bad (Effects unknown)
- May act similarly to the above stat decrease, but with specific skills. Currently, no Skill with this icon exists.
- Skill occurs after a set number of Enemies are killed.
- Skill occurs after a set amount of Gold is collected.
- Familiar (Effects unknown)
- Currently, no Skill that summons or revolves around familiars exists.
- Skill can occur a max of 2 times.
- Skill can occur a max of 3 times.
- Skill can occur a max of 4 times.
- Skill can occur a max of 5 times.
- Skill can occur a max of 6 times.
- Skill can occur a max of 7 times.
- Skill can occur a max of 8 times.
- Skill can occur a max of 9 times.
- Skill occurs every level up.
- Skill occurs whenever the player takes damage.
- Skill occurs whenever the player is revived.
- Skill occurs whenever the player's health reaches a critical level.
- Skill occurs when the player overheals by a set amount.
- Skill occurs when the player skips a level up.
- Skill grants a weapon.
- Special Skills, including stat boosts with no condition.
## List of Effects
Currently, 24 characters have Survarot cards associated with them, often mirroring that character's stats and passives.

| 023 | Divano Thelma | +20% Curse +20% Luck | Start Gaining +1 Armor every 5 levels (Max 5 times). Start gaining +1 Banish every level. | Triggers a Rosary when Skipping a Level Up.
| 024 | Zi'Assunta Belpaese | +6% Area, Duration, Might, and Speed | Start gaining +0.5% Might, Speed, Duration, and Area every level. | Gains +1 passive weapon slot after gaining a set amount of gold [2.5k, 7.5k, 15k].
## Enemahs
After obtaining the  Enemahs relic from Big Troubler in The Lycaeum, a set of 18 new cards will be added to the pool. These cards are based on various enemies, and trade off one stat for another. Note that all Enemahs have the same GALA skill, being +1%  Might every level. INVE Enemahs will not have their bonuses halved. Instead, they are solely inverted. POLY will double both the buff and debuff of these cards.
| Card Number | Card and Name | Beneficial Stat | Tradeoff
| E01 | Mighty Swordian | +200% Might | -2 Amount
| E02 | Lost Twin | -30% Cooldown | -30% Area
| E03 | Giant Mantichana | +30% Area | -30% Might
| E04 | Jellyfish Swarm | +3 Amount | +30% Cooldown
| E05 | Archon Ascia | +3 Armor | -30% Move Speed
| E06 | Milk Elemental | +400 Max Health | -0.5 Recovery
| E07 | Musc Musc | +1.0 Recovery | -3 Armor
| E08 | Skelewing | +40% Move Speed | -40 Max Health
| E09 | Sig.Ra Rossi | +100% Speed | -50% Duration
| E10 | Merdusa | +100% Duration | -50% Speed
| E11 | Space Pickle | +100% Luck | -50% Greed
| E12 | Skelegem | +100% Growth | -50% Luck
| E13 | Sphon'Dato | +100% Greed | -50% Growth
| E14 | Menta Elemental | +100% Magnet | -50% Curse
| E15 | Skullone | +100% Curse | -3 Revival
| E16 | Skullorosso | +3 Revival | -50% Magnet
| E17 | Fallen Cherubbello | +50 Charm +50% Fever | -500 Invul Time
| E18 | Cosmic Egg | +500 Invul Time | -50 Charm -50% Fever
## List of FOIL Skills
Whenever a card appears with the Edition, it is granted a random Skill. FOIL Skills can be grouped by condition. FOIL Skills will be shown on the card prior to obtainment, being the very bottom Skill shown.
 Skill occurs when the player  Skips a level up.
- Temporary -100%  Cooldown.
- Fully recovery HP and increase  Max Health by +40.
- Trigger a  Rosary.
- Trigger an  Orologion.
 Skill occurs whenever the player is revived.
- Trigger a  Rosary.
- -20%  Curse.
- Trigger a  Rapid Fire.
- Trigger a  Vacuum.
 Skill occurs whenever the player takes damage.
- Gain a stacking 10-second +0.1  Recovery bonus.
- Gain a stacking 10-second +1  Armor bonus.
- Gain 1 gold coin.
- Retaliate with a Ground Slam.
- This effect is similar to  Torino and  Eskizzibur.
 Special Skills, including stat boosts with no condition.
- +100% Critical Damage
- +50  Charm
- +15%  Defang
- Nearby allies gain a +1  Armor and +0.3  Recovery Bonus.
 Skill occurs after a set number of enemies are killed.
- +1  Amount after defeating [1k, 5k, 10k] enemies.
- +1  Revival after defeating [1k, 5k, 10k] enemies.
- +1  Armor after defeating [1k, 5k, 10k] enemies.
- Start a  Gold Fever after defeating [1k, 3k, 5k] enemies.
- Gain 100 gold coins after defeating [1k, 2k, 3k] enemies.
- This is granted via a  Rich Coin Bag, meaning it can trigger a Gold Fever when paired with  Disco of Gold (XV).
 Skill occurs after a set amount of gold is collected.
- Spawns a ring of light sources after gaining [500, 1k, 2k] gold.
- Gains +1 passive weapon slot after gaining [2.5k, 7.5k, 15k] gold.
- Gains +1  Revival after gaining [1k, 5k, 10k] gold.
- Gains +0.5 Thorns after gaining [1k, 2k, 3k] gold.
 Skill occurs whenever the player's health reaches a critical level.
- Trigger an  Nduja Fritta Tanto.
- +50  Armor for 10 seconds.
- Gain 40  Max Health and recover 30% Health
 Skill occurs when the player overheals by a set amount.
- Overhealing by +16 increases  Gold Finger and  Gold Fever duration by +1%. (Grants +1%  Fever)
- Overhealing by +16 triggers a  Sorbetto.
- Overhealing by +16 grants +1  Reroll.
- Overhealing by +16 grants +3%  Might.
## RISK
Drafting Survarots will accumulate RISK, increasing the enemies’ Health and Damage with each card. RISK can be lowered by picking cards, trading some of the player’s power for weaker enemies.
- Note that the Edition also halves the Starting Bonus of a Survarot, instead of just inverting its stats. This, in combination with the general power provided by Survarots, makes taking cards more beneficial than it may initially appear, especially if the player has hit any Stat Caps.
In Party Mode, and both forms of co-op, RISK is shared between all party members/players, accumulating based on the total number of Survarots between the party.
The amount of RISK gained depends on how many Survarots the player has taken, adding to the overall difficulty modifier, 'D'.
| Card Number | Multiplier added
| 0,1 | +0.20
| 2,3 | +0.15
| 4,5 | +0.10
| 6+ | +(0.05+0.01*Number of Survarots)
- Thus, the 6th Survarot will add +0.11 to the multiplier, the 7th will add +0.12, and so on.
The following formula is used to calculate the "Enemy Difficulty Multiplier." This bonus is applied directly to enemy health, but only half of the bonus is applies to enemy damage.
Enemy Difficulty Multiplier=1+Total Multiplier1+INVECards 
When Survarots are enabled, enemies will have a health increase of +20% and a damage increase of +10% before the player takes any Survarots. 
- If the player only takes INVE Survarots, the first card taken will not add to RISK. After two cards, RISK will slowly start to decrease past the base RISK.
## Accumulating Levels
Survarots accumulate levels as the player levels up, normally at a 1:1 ratio. This accumulation only begins after the card is drafted, and does not apply retroactively.
The number of levels a card gets each time the player levels up is increased by the card’s/player’s Survarot Mult. Every source of Survarot Mult is additive with each other.
- This is mainly utilized the Edition, which grants a Survarot Multiplier of 4.
- Whenever the player levels up, that card will gain 4 levels instead of 1.
-  Jimbo [DLC] has an intrinsic x4 Survarot Multiplier via his passive.
-  Secretino Dagsson is also able to get up to a x10 Survarot Multiplier via his passive.
Accumulating extra levels allows for certain effects to happen quicker or occur multiple times each level up, since all level-based Survarot effects are dependent on the card’s level.
- This is especially potent with Skills that grant stats every level up, such as  Imelda Belpaese’s Survarot granting up to +10%  Growth every level up with a Survarot Multiplier of x10 on  Secretino Dagsson.
- Also, for example, with an x4 Survarot Multiplier, the player can obtain the weapons granted by the Editions of  Mortaccio,  Yatta Cavallo,  Bianca Ramba, and  O'Sole Meeo's Survarot in 20 levels instead of 80.
## Tips
- If the player is having a hard time dealing with a horde of enemies, they can pick up the Menta Elemental enemah. In this context, the increase in  Magnet and the reduction of  Curse can be both beneficial to the player.
- Likewise, in this situation, they should avoid picking up Skullone enemah, as the increase in  Curse and reduction in number of  Revivals would both be detrimental to the player.
- If the player has the  Edge of the Earth (VIII) equipped, they can freely pickup the Dommario survarot and the Archon Ascia enemah without worrying of decreased move speed.
- Likewise, the Krochi Freetto survarot will not provide a movement speed buff and only be beneficial through its skills. Furthermore, the Archon Ascia enemah would only decrease  Max Health.
## Trivia
- "Survarot" is a portmanteau of "Survivor" and "Tarot".
- The card editions, , , and , are a direct reference to the polychrome, holographic, and foil card editions from Balatro.
- The card edition is also a reference to the negative edition from the same game, but had its name changed.
- This makes the only edition originally from Vampire Survivors.

# The Reaper/Other Strategies
Below are old strategies used to kill Reapers, before the introduction of Infinite Corridor. They are no longer advised and may no longer work, but are still recorded here for legacy purposes.
## Survival
Runs that aim to survive as long as possible are built around trying to keep the reapers frozen in time using Clock Lancet and prolonging your life with Laurel and Revivals. Cooldown is critical for the strategy, as it allows the Lancet and Laurel to proc more often. Duration is also important because it prolongs the freeze effect from Lancet.
Recommended characters:
- Arca
- Christine
Recommended items:
- Clock Lancet
- Laurel
- Empty Tome
- Spellbinder
- Tiragisú
## Killing Reaper
Killing the Reaper through normal means is near impossible. Aside from his massive health pool (655,350 X player level) he has insane DPS - and more of them keep coming after you every minute.
However, the following methods are known to work:
- Using Suor Clerici's initial +400% Area bonus.
- You don't need to alter any game files for this method.
- You need to place yourself somewhat "pixel-perfect" to pull it off.
- Using MissingN▯'s potential to have invulnerability.
- You do need to alter your game files (savegame, see linked article) to unlock this character, which some might consider "cheating".
- No special placement is necessary for it to work.
- Using Toastie's level 200 passive bonus, Awake Arcana, Tiragisú, Revival PowerUp, Armor (both item and PowerUp) and high damage weapon (e.g., Gatti Amari).
- Using Mortaccio's Bone to kill the Reaper. Similar to Suor Clerici, you stay at low level.
Method 1. and 2. rely on the fact that the reaper spawns with "low" health with a low character level at the 30 minute mark; method 3. rely on the massive Health and Armor passive bonus Toastie gains at level 200, along with extra Armor stat which gains from PowerUp, item, full used Awake Arcana, to tank the Reaper's damage, then use high damage weapon to killed it.
## Using Suor Clerici
- First remove all Magnet upgrades from your character. Growth may also be removed to reduce the likelihood of hitting level 3 accidentally.
- Start with Clerici in inlaid library
- Collect the green XP gem on the table for your first upgrade.
- If your first upgrade is not Runetracer, reset the run and try again.Step 5 position
- Stay behind the chair and table where you found the gem. A good position has Clerici's belt above the floor line in the top of the two wooden strips, and the right hand (her left hand) on the same level on the x-axis as the left end of the wooden frame of the picture.
- Do not collect any XP on the ground, so that the area of your Runetracer is bigger than your character sprite.
- If you do not see a red XP gem on the ground by roughly the 5 minute mark, it may be advised to restart the run. The red gem will be off-screen in this case and you will have to guess where it is. It may also be too far away to pick up before it kills you. With the Revival PowerUp it can still be possible to reach it, however.
- Stay inside your stuck Runetracer until the 30:00 min mark.
- After it spawns (it must be "after", as Death's hit points are set based on your current level...you want that as low as possible so Death's hit points are also low), run towards the red XP gem on the ground to gain around 100 levels.
- Items:
- Get: Santa Water, Clock Lancet, Laurel, Runetracer, Bracer, Candelabrador, Spellbinder, Empty Tome
- Optional: Tiragisú, Attractorb, Magic Wand, Knife, Duplicator, Cross, Bone, Spinach
- Avoid: Lightning Ring, Fire Wand
- Additional equipment might include Bracer, which can increase the DPS of Bone and Runetracer by making them bounce faster, and Duplicator, which further increases that DPS. Tiragisú may not increase DPS, but can help save the run if something goes wrong. It is also advised to avoid weapons that can hit random enemies like Fire Wand and Lightning Ring, and instead focus on weapons like Magic Wand and Cross to fill the last slots, as they hit the enemy closest to you. The reasoning for this is that additional spawns of it will likely get stuck on the library shelves. If they get hit by your weapons, however, there is a chance they will break free, because of death's negative knockback pushing him towards you. Having weapons with projectiles that stay close to you can help avoid this scenario.
- Lead it back to behind the chairs and table. A good position is standing slightly above the right chair, with Clerici's belt roughly in line with the floor line, and a little bit away from the right bookshelf, to the point that your Laurel shield barely touches it.
[All-in-one Reaper kill guide (by NeverNathaniel)]All-in-one Reaper kill guide (by NeverNathaniel)
- This was shown by the Reddit user RivenMarryMePls in a post on the Vampire Survivors subreddit and first confirmed in this video, Youtuber NeverNathaniel made a detailed video to explain how it works. This video insists that you must pickup and level empty tome to max before all other weapons; however as of February 19, 2022, a bug that caused empty tome's passive effect to not apply to leveled weapons has been fixed and this is no longer necessary.
## Riskier variant
This method is almost identical to the previous method, but involves picking up the Empty Tome as the 7th passive item.
- Instead of grabbing the green gem from the table, head left towards the nearest Empty Tome.
- This is easier with the Milky Way Map acquired from the Dairy Plant stage, as you may restart if desired until the tome is a reasonable distance from spawn.
- Avoid experience gems, and ensure that you are at level 2 (with Runetracer) when at the Empty Tome.
- Do not pick up the Empty Tome, but position yourself in the same location as in the previous method.
- Wait until 30:00.
- Grab the red gem like in the previous method.
- Do not pick up the Empty Tome until you have finished leveling up, otherwise it triggers a bug where the level-up dialog gets frozen.
- The Empty Tome should be leveled with remaining chests.
## Using MissingN▯
## TL;DR
Using MissingN▯, you must be invulnerable and reach the Experience Gem drop limit. Afterwards you can idle until the Reaper spawns.
## Video
https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3WT6XA-rjCE
## Prerequisite
Unlock MissingN▯ as described in his article.
Good stats for a Reaper kill run
## Rolling stats
Take a closer look at the randomized stats and how far their range goes. They are altered every time you press "START" in the main menu, which in combination with Escape (Esc on your keyboard) can bring up plenty of different versions of him within a few seconds.
- Max Health must be negative - this grants invulnerability!
- The fastest method is therefore to eyeball the Max Health value while rolling stats, and only then consider the other stats.
- Move Speed, Might, Area, Cooldown, Growth and Curse should be in a range that makes the game behave at least somewhat "as usual".
- Aside from their DPS downside...
- Close-to-0 Move Speed means it can take you up to several minutes to even pick up the exp gems after having reached minute 30.
- Low Area exponentially increases your chance of mobs closing in, making you pick up exp gems.
- High Cooldown also increases this chance.
- Low Growth can grind leveling up at minute 30 way down or even to a halt. You still need to kill the Reaper!
- Low Curse can drastically lower the amount of enemies that spawn.
- While that sounds somewhat useful, while testing it even lead to zero red gems spawning in a test run.
- There's one red gem dropping rather late that gives something around 75 levels at once, drastically increasing your DPS - missing that is just not advisable.
## Stage settings
See Stage.
- Pick Hurry for a general speedup of the real world time you have to invest.
- Pick Hyper if you need to adjust your Move Speed (e.g. advisable if you rolled too close to 0)
## Experience gem drop limit
Experience Gems have a limit on how many can be present at once. Once you reach this limit, rather than dropping a new gem enemies will increase the experience of the existing gem furthest away from you (becoming the "red gem" when its total experience gets high enough).
Once you reach this limit and the mobs cease dropping them, it's now safe to go idle until minute 30 without leveling up by accident.
## Initial setup
MissingN▯ has a 50% chance to start with Death Spiral - in that case you don't need to move at all since all mobs will die way before they reach you.
Otherwise, you can either try to outmaneuver the mobs with Axe (starting weapon). Yet you will probably pick up one or two level ups worth of gems. In this case, prioritize getting a proper AoE-in-all-directions weapon like Whip or Garlic to put a stop to this asap! Depending on your rolls, King Bible, Santa Water, Runetracer, Lightning Ring, Phiera Der Tuphello, Eight The Sparrow, Song of Mana, Cherry Bomb and Carréllo might be valid alternatives as well. The remainders, like Knife, present quite a hassle and should be avoided.
## Idling
Once the exp gem limit has been reached, there is nothing to do until the Reaper spawns.
## Kill Reaper
After Reaper has spawned, pick up all Experience Gems. In general you should choose high direct-damage weapons like Whip, Magic Wand, Knife, Bone.
If desired, you can also try and "abuse" the bouncing effect from Runetracer in close quarters (somewhere around the table mentioned the first variant on this page). This requires special positioning though.
Depending on your DPS and level the first Reaper spawned with, you will diminish his HP to zero at some point. Even with very good rolls this is not going to happen before five, six or even more Deaths are present though. This means we require a method to check when we have reached our goal.
## Determine if a Reaper has died
Distinctive DPS overhead values
Take note of the kill counter at minute 30.
Once the Reaper dies, it will increase by 1.
the Reaper dying
Note
The Reaper does not drop Golden Eggs nor summon White Hand if killed without Infinite Corridor or Crimson Shroud.

# Tides of the Foscari
This article is about the DLC. For other uses, see Tides of the Foscari (disambiguation).
| Tides of the Foscari
| 
| Expansion
| Release date | 13 April 2023 (Update)
| Platform(s) | PC, Xbox, PlayStation, Nintendo Switch, iOS, Android
| Retail price | $1.99
| {"image":"Tides of the Foscari.png","name":"Tides of the Foscari","release":"13 April 2023","price":"$1.99","update":"New DLC & Patch 1.4.0","platform":"PC, Xbox, PlayStation, Nintendo Switch, iOS, Android"}
Nestled deep within a continent-sized forest is Foscari Academy, a school where the elite of the world train their children for careers as powerful wizards, indefatigable generals, and expert spies. The Academy is divided into three houses, a premise hitherto untouched in all of fantasy fiction. Three students, one from each house, brave the journey into a forest stuffed with mythological creatures, all to have a jolly adventure, with absolutely nothing interrupting the whimsy. This is all mind-blowingly original stuff, we know, but it only gets better folks.
Official description
Tides of the Foscari is the second expansion for Vampire Survivors, released on April 13, 2023. It introduces two new stages, Lake Foscari and Abyss Foscari, as well as new characters, monsters, and weapons. 
## Features
- 8 new characters:
-  Eleanor Uziron - Star pupil of The Azure Tower and a skilled magic user, Eleanor is as bright as she is shy.
-  Maruto Cuts - Undefeated champion of The Crimson Anvil. Stern, brooding, but with a heart of gold beneath his scowl, Maruto is a man of few words and strong arm.
-  Keitha Muort - Sly, quick-witted, and fun loving, Keitha will do anything for a bit of adventure and levity. Deliberately stays near the bottom of class rankings in The Amber Sickle to avoid hassle.
-  Luminaire Foscari - The guardian of Lake Foscari, Luminaire is an ancient witch of great power and pure heart. Known in stories as the Lady of the Lake, or sometimes the Arboreal Mistress.
-  Genevieve Gruyère
-  Je-Ne-Viv
-  Sammy
-  Rottin'Ghoul
- 13 new weapons:
-  SpellString - Eleanor’s trusted textbook, sure to provide the answers on any magical topic that might cross her path.
-  SpellStream
-  SpellStrike
-  SpellStrom
-  Eskizzibur - Forged by Maruto’s blacksmith uncle as a jape, but to his surprise his young nephew was capable of lifting it.
-  Legionnaire
-  Flash Arrow - Keitha’s home crafted arrows will put a hole through a tree at 100 paces, though her prodigious tendon strength certainly helps.
-  Millionaire
-  Prismatic Missile - A fragment of concentrated elemental power loaned to Luminaire by spirits as thanks for her continued protection of the natural world.
-  Luminaire
-  Shadow Servant( Silver Sliver)
-  Ophion
-  Party Popper( Party Pooper)
- 1 new passive item:
-  Academy Badge
- 7 new music tracks:
- "Tides of the Foscari"
- "An Introduction to Sorcery"
- "Slaying Dragons"
- "Hide and Steal"
- "Ashes of the Banished"
- "The Heart of the World"
- "The World Eater"
- 2 new stages:
- Lake Foscari - An enchanted forest stuffed with fae mysteries, mythological entities, and beasts you’d find in any given RPG bestiary. The perfect place to have an average adventure with zero complications.
- Abyss Foscari - Sealed by Luminaire in ancient times to contain the darkness within, these underground ruins are now accessible, but so is the outside world to the evil from the depths.
- 1 new adventure
- 1 new relic:
- Lake Foscari Map

# Tileset
A tileset is a term used to describe a square or a rectangle, shown on the  Milky Way Map, which corresponds to a full tilemap.
In some unlimited stages, such as Mad Forest, if the player moves out of a specific tileset, there will be a repeated tileset in the player's given direction, with stage items and pickups remaining in place in their original tileset. However, in some limited stages, such as The Lycaeum and Carlo Cart, the entire stage takes place in a single tileset, and among some limited stages, the player could teleport from one place or bound to another within this tileset.

# Wall Clipping
Not to be confused with Flight.
Wall clipping is a game mechanic that allows for a player character to move out of the wall boundaries in a stage. It can be achieved if the player character has enough move speed and/or continually attempts to go through a wall or a corner. Another factor that may contribute to the success of wall clipping is the player's FPS (frames per second), as if the player experiences lag, then the act of wall clipping may be more successful.[Verify]
The process of wall clipping while using a character with the flight mechanic, such as  Gyoruntin, is easier and requires less effort to do so.
